<div class='question question'>

Work out the following sum

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
     & 1   &  3    & 8    & 4  \\
{}+  &     &  6    & 4    & 2 \\
\hline\\
\hline
\end{array} 
$

- $1026$
- $2006$  
- $2026$
- $226$
- $1926$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
     & 1   &  3    & 8    & 4  \\
{}+  &     &  6    & 4    & 2 \\
\hline
    & 2    &  0    & 2    & 6
\end{array} 
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$2026$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

{}+  &     &  6    & 4    & 2 \\
\hline
    & 2    &  0    & 2    & 6
\end{array} 
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>C</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Find $30 \%$ of $120$

- $30$
- $3.6$
- $36$ 
- $40$ 
- $64$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 30 \% \ \text{of} \ 120 \\\\
&= \dfrac{30}{100} \times 120 \\\\
&= \dfrac{30 \times 120}{100} \\\\
&= \dfrac{3 \cancel{0} \times 12 \cancel{0}}{1 \cancel{0} \cancel{0}} \\\\
&= 3 \times 12  \\\\
&= 36
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 30 \% \ \text{of} \ 120 \\\\
&= \dfrac{30}{100} \times 120 \\\\
&= \dfrac{30 \times 120}{100} \\\\
&= \dfrac{3 \cancel{0} \times 12 \cancel{0}}{1 \cancel{0} \cancel{0}} \\\\
&= 3 \times 12  \\\\
&= 36
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>C</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$36$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

- $46 \degree$
- $316 \degree$
- $136 \degree$
- $36 \degree$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
44 + a     &= 180  \\\\
a          &= 180 - 44 \\\\
a          &= 136 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

What is the missing number?

$560 \div \boxed{ \phantom{70} } = 8$

- $7$
- $70$
- $700$
- $0.7$
- $0.007$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
560 \div a          &= 8 \\\\
\dfrac{560} {a}     &= 8 \\\\
560                 &= 8a \\\\
8a                  &= 560 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {560} {8} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {8 \times 70} {8} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {8} \times 70} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
a                   &= 70
\end{aligned}
$

Missing number $= 70$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$70$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the missing number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
560 \div a          &= 8 \\\\
\dfrac{560} {a}     &= 8 \\\\
560                 &= 8a \\\\
8a                  &= 560 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {560} {8} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {8 \times 70} {8} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {8} \times 70} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
a                   &= 70
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Missing number $= 70$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$70$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

The local bakery sells muffins at $85 \text{p}$ each. I have $\pounds 15$ to spend. How many muffins can I buy? 

- $17$
- $16$
- $15$
- $14$
- $20$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required
.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Count of muffins}    &= \dfrac {\text {Total money}} {\text {Cost of one muffin}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\pounds 15} {85 \text {p}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1500 \text {p}} {85 \text {p}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {5 \times 300} {5 \times 17} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 300} {\cancel {5} \times 17} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {300} {17} \\\\
                            &= 17 \ \text {remainder} \ 11 \\\\
                            &\approx 17
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$17$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_no_dot_note.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Count of muffins}    &= \dfrac {\text {Total money}} {\text {Cost of one muffin}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\pounds 15} {85 \text {p}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1500 \text {p}} {85 \text {p}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {5 \times 300} {5 \times 17} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 300} {\cancel {5} \times 17} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {300} {17} \\\\
                            &= 17 \ \text {remainder} \ 11 \\\\
                            &\approx 17
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$17$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

What is two-fifths of $45$?

- $9$
- $18$
- $27$
- $5$
- $10$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{2}{5} \text{ of } 45 \\\\
&= \dfrac{2}{5} \times 45 \\\\
&= \dfrac{2 \times 45}{5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2 \times 9 \times 5}{5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2 \times 9 \times \cancel{5}}{\cancel{5}} \\\\
&= 2 \times 9 \\\\
&= 18
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{2}{5} \text{ of } 45 \\\\
&= \dfrac{2}{5} \times 45 \\\\
&= \dfrac{2 \times 45}{5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2 \times 9 \times 5}{5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2 \times 9 \times \cancel{5}}{\cancel{5}} \\\\
&= 2 \times 9 \\\\
&= 18
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$18$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

The area of the parallelogram is $38 \ \text{cm}^2$. Find the length of its base.

![streatham-and-clapham-high-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-13-00.png](/assets/streatham-and-clapham-high-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-13-00.png "streatham-and-clapham-high-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-13-00.png")

- $9.5 \ \text{cm}$
- $8.5 \ \text{cm}$
- $7.5 \ \text{cm}$
- $7.25 \ \text{cm}$
- $9.25 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of parallelogram} = \text{base} \times \text{height}$


Let the base of the parallelogram $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times b                    &= 38 \\\\
b                             &= \dfrac {38} {4} \\\\
b                             &= \dfrac {4 \times 9.5} {4} \\\\
b                             &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 9.5} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
b                             &= 9.5 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of the base of the parallelogram $= 9.5 \ \text{cm}$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$9.5 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_parallelogram_expanded
<emptyline>
Let the base of the parallelogram $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times b                    &= 38 \\\\
b                             &= \dfrac {38} {4} \\\\
b                             &= \dfrac {4 \times 9.5} {4} \\\\
b                             &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 9.5} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
b                             &= 9.5 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Length of the base of the parallelogram $= 9.5 \ \text{cm}$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9.5 \ \text{cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='review-comments'>

<h4>Review Comments</h4>
line 1, 2, 3: Delete                                                                                                                                                              line 4: change : Area of Parallelogram = Base $\times$ Height.                                                                                          write a proper katex with $\begin{aligned}                                                                        base \times height &= area of paralellogram                                               no need to take base as a leave it as base only and solve
</div>

\begin{aligned}
&=  340 \ \text{millimetres} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{340}{1000} \ \text{m} \\\\

\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

- $40$

<div class='workings'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average score}    &= \dfrac {48 + 37 + 40 + 48 + 36 + 31} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {240} {6} \\\\

                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 40} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                        &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$13, \quad 9, \quad 5, \quad 1, \quad -3, \quad \ldots$

What are the next two numbers in the sequence?

- $-1, -5$
- $-6, -9$
- $-8, -12$
- $-7, -11$
- $0, -4$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by subtracting $4$ from the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC{(-4)}} 
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(-4)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(-4)}}  
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(-4)}}   
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(-4)}}   
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(-4)}}  \\
13,  &&    9,  &&  5,  &&   1,  &&   -3, && \ldots, && \ldots
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{First missing number}    &= -3 - 4 \\\\
                               &= -7 \\\\\
\text{Second missing number}   &= -7 - 4 \\\\
                               &= -11
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>D</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$-7, -11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note
<emptyline>
The next term is calculated by subtracting $4$ from the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:
<emptyline>
$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(-4)}} 
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(-4)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(-4)}}  
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(-4)}}   
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(-4)}}   
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(-4)}}  \\
13,  &&    9,  &&  5,  &&   1,  &&   -3, && \ldots, && \ldots
\end{matrix}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{First missing number}    &= -3 - 4 \\\\
                               &= -7 \\\\\
\text{Second missing number}   &= -7 - 4 \\\\
                               &= -11
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>D</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$-7, -11$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the rule of the above sequence?

- $4n - 17$
- $17 - 4n$
- $-7n + 13$
- $5 - 3n$
- $9n + 4$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

This is an arithmetic sequence and the difference between the consecutive patterns of the sequence is constant.

The next terms is calculated by adding multiple of $-4$ to the first term and the number of times it is added is based on the position in the sequence.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 13 + (-4) \times (n-1)\\\\
&= 13 - 4n + 4 \\\\
&= 13 + 4 - 4n \\\\
&= 17 - 4n
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$17 - 4n$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>This is an arithmetic sequence and the difference between the consecutive patterns of the sequence is constant.
<emptyline>
The next terms is calculated by adding multiple of $-4$ to the first term and the number of times it is added is based on the position in the sequence.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 13 + (-4) \times (n-1)\\\\
&= 13 - 4n + 4 \\\\
&= 13 + 4 - 4n \\\\
&= 17 - 4n
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$17 - 4n$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question question'>

I think of a number, multiply by $2$ and add $5$. The answer is $19$. What was the number?

- $6$
- $8$
- $7$
- $48$
- $28$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + 5  &= 19 \\\\
2a              &= 19 - 5 \\\\
2a              &= 14 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{14} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {2 \times 7} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 7} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a               &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + 5  &= 19 \\\\
2a              &= 19 - 5 \\\\
2a              &= 14 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{14} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {2 \times 7} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 7} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a               &= 7
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>C</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Pens cost $35 \text{p}$ each. How much money will a box of $25$ pens cost?

- $\pounds 8.40$
- $\pounds 8.75$
- $\pounds 87.50$
- $\pounds 865.00$
- $\pounds 9.25$    

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of } 25 \text{ pens}   &= 25 \times 35 \\\\
                                  &= 875  \text{p} \\\\
                                  &= \pounds 8.75
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 8.75$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of } 25 \text{ pens}   &= 25 \times 35 \\\\
                                  &= 875  \text{p} \\\\
                                  &= \pounds 8.75
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 8.75$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

I leave home at $7.35 \ \text{am}$ and it takes me $42 \ \text{minutes}$ to get to school. 
What time do I get to school?

- $8.17 \ \text{am}$
- $08{:}16$
- $18{:}07$
- $18{:}17$
- $8.07 \ \text{am}$  

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time}     &= 7{:}35 \ \text{am} + 42 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                &= 8{:}17 \ \text{am}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$8{:}17 \ \text{am}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time}     &= 7{:}35 \ \text{am} + 42 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                &= 8{:}17 \ \text{am}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8{:}17 \ \text{am}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

There is a sale at the local mobile phone shop. 
Alex wants to buy a new phone which originally cost $\pounds 150$. 
The prices of all the phones have been reduced by $20 \%$.

What amount will Alex have to pay for his new phone?

- $135.00$
- $\pounds 149.70$
- $\pounds 147.00$
- $\pounds 130.00$
- $\pounds 120.00$  

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

If the price is reduced by $20 \%$, then the new price will be $100 - 20 = 80 \%$ of the original price. Using that, we get

$
\begin{aligned} 
\text{Sale price}       &= 80 \% \text{ of } 150 \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {80} {100} \times 150  \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {80 \times 150} {100} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {8 \cancel{0} \times 15 \cancel{0}} {1 \cancel{0} \cancel{0}} \\\\
                        &= 8 \times 15 \\\\
                        &= \pounds 120
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{New price}            &= \text {Original price} - \text {Discount} \\\\
                            &= 150 - 20 \% \text{ of } 150  \\\\
                            &= 150 - \dfrac {20} {100} \times 150 \\\\
                            &= 150 - \dfrac {20 \times 150} {100} \\\\
                            &= 150 - \dfrac {2 \cancel 0 \times 15 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
                            &= 150 - 2 \times 15 \\\\
                            &= 150 - 30 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 120
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>E</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 120$ 

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>If the price is reduced by $20 \%$, then the new price will be $100 - 20 = 80 \%$ of the original price. Using that, we get
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned} 
\text{Sale price}       &= 80 \% \text{ of } 150 \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {80} {100} \times 150  \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {80 \times 150} {100} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {8 \cancel{0} \times 15 \cancel{0}} {1 \cancel{0} \cancel{0}} \\\\
                        &= 8 \times 15 \\\\
                        &= \pounds 120
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{New price}            &= \text {Original price} - \text {Discount} \\\\
                            &= 150 - 20 \% \text{ of } 150  \\\\
                            &= 150 - \dfrac {20} {100} \times 150 \\\\
                            &= 150 - \dfrac {20 \times 150} {100} \\\\
                            &= 150 - \dfrac {2 \cancel 0 \times 15 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
                            &= 150 - 2 \times 15 \\\\
                            &= 150 - 30 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 120
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>E</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 120$ 
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

A bag of Fruit Pastillies has $15$ red, $20$ orange, $12$ yellow and $13$ black sweets. 
You choose a sweet from the bag without looking inside.  

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the probablility that the sweet you choose will be an orange sweet?

- $\dfrac{1}{3}$

- $\dfrac{1}{6}$

- $\dfrac{5}{6}$

- $\dfrac{4}{5}$

- $\dfrac{1}{5}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(orange)}        &= \dfrac {20} {15 + 20 + 12 + 13} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {20} {60} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {20} {3\times 20} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {20}} {3\times \cancel {20}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {3}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{1}{3}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_probability
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(orange)}        &= \dfrac {20} {15 + 20 + 12 + 13} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {20} {60} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {20} {3\times 20} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {20}} {3\times \cancel {20}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {3}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac{1}{3}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the probability that it is not a yellow sweet?

- $\dfrac{1}{3}$

- $\dfrac{1}{6}$

- $\dfrac{5}{6}$

- $\dfrac{4}{5}$

- $\dfrac{1}{5}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(not yellow)} &= \dfrac {60 - 12} {60} \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {48} {60} \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {4 \times 12} {5 \times 12} \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {12}} {5\times \cancel {12}} \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {4} {5}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>D</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{4}{5}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_probability
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(not yellow)} &= \dfrac {60 - 12} {60} \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {48} {60} \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {4 \times 12} {5 \times 12} \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {12}} {5\times \cancel {12}} \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {4} {5}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>D</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac{4}{5}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

$4d + 7e - d + 2e =$

- $12de$

- $3d + 9e$
- $5d - 9e$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned} 
&=  4d + 7e - d + 2e \\\\
&=  4d -d + 7e + 2e \\\\
&=  3d + 9e
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>D</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$3d + 9e$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned} 
&=  4d + 7e - d + 2e \\\\
&=  4d -d + 7e + 2e \\\\
&=  3d + 9e
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

$3.2 \times 2.6 =$

- $8.32$
- $6.12$
- $9.12$
- $6.32$
- $5.12$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Let's first multiply the numbers ignoring the decimal points.


$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
                      & 3     & 2     \\
{}\times              & 2     & 6     \\
\hline 
                1     & 9     & 2     \\
                6     & 4     & 0     \\
\hline 
                8     & 3     & 2     \\
\end{array}
$

Between the two numbers, the total number of digits after the decimal point is two.

Let's account for the decimal point now. 

$ = 8.32$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$8.32$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_multiply_when_decimal_points_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
                      & 3     & 2     \\
{}\times              & 2     & 6     \\
\hline 
                1     & 9     & 2     \\
                6     & 4     & 0     \\
\hline 
                8     & 3     & 2     \\
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Between the two numbers, the total number of digits after the decimal point is two.
<emptyline>
Let's account for the decimal point now. 
<emptyline>
$ = 8.32$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8.32$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Write three-fifths as a percentage.

- $6 \%$
- $30 \%$
- $35 \%$
- $3 \%$
- $60 \%$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{3}{5} \times 100 \\\\
&= \dfrac{3 \times 100}{5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{3 \times 20 \times 5}{5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{3 \times 20 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
&= 3 \times 20 \\\\
&= 60 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>E</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$60 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{3}{5} \times 100 \\\\
&= \dfrac{3 \times 100}{5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{3 \times 20 \times 5}{5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{3 \times 20 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
&= 3 \times 20 \\\\
&= 60 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>E</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$60 \%$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

&= \dfrac{121 \times \cancel{5}}{\cancel{5} \times 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac{121}{100} \\\\
&= 1.21
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

\begin{aligned}

C   &=  5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

$\dfrac{2}{5} + \dfrac{3}{5} - \dfrac{1}{10}$

- $\dfrac{6}{5}$
- $\dfrac{9}{10}$
- $\dfrac{4}{10}$
- $\dfrac{3}{10}$
- $\dfrac{4}{5}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&=  \dfrac{2}{5} + \dfrac{3}{5} - \dfrac{1}{10} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{2 \times 2}{5 \times 2 } + \dfrac{3 \times 2}{5 \times 2 } - \dfrac{1}{10} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{4}{10} + \dfrac{6}{10} - \dfrac{1}{10} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{4 + 6 - 1} {10} \\\\
&=   \dfrac{9}{10}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{9}{10}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&=  \dfrac{2}{5} + \dfrac{3}{5} - \dfrac{1}{10} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{2 \times 2}{5 \times 2 } + \dfrac{3 \times 2}{5 \times 2 } - \dfrac{1}{10} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{4}{10} + \dfrac{6}{10} - \dfrac{1}{10} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{4 + 6 - 1} {10} \\\\
&=   \dfrac{9}{10}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac{9}{10}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

![streatham-and-clapham-high-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-1-00.png](/assets/streatham-and-clapham-high-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-1-00.png "streatham-and-clapham-high-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-1-00.png")  

This is a magic square.  

All the columns, rows and diagonals add up to $30$.  

Several numbers have been missed out.

What number replaces $B$?

- $6$
- $8$
- $7$
- $5$
- $10$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

|         | column $1$ | column $2$ | column $3$ |
|---------|------------|------------|------------|
| row $1$ | $9$        | $A$        | $R1C3$     |
| row $2$ | $R2C1$     | $10$       | $R2C3$     |
| row $3$ | $13$       | $B$        | $R3C3$     |

To find $B$ we need to first find the value of R3C3.

As per the question, solving diagonally,

$
\begin{aligned}
R3C3 &= 30 - 9 - 10 \\\\
     &= 21 - 10 \\\\
     &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

Now lets find value of $B$, solving Row $3$

$
\begin{aligned}
13 + B + 11 &= 30 \\\\
24 + B      &= 30 \\\\
B           &= 30 - 24 \\\\
B           &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

The number that replaces $B = 6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

What number replaces $A$?

- $12$ 
- $13$  
- $14$
- $15$
- $16$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>

|         | column $1$ | column $2$ | column $3$ |

| row $2$ | $R2C1$     | $10$       | $R2C3$     |
| row $3$ | $13$       | $B$        | $R3C3$     |

To find $A$ we need to first find the value of R1C3.

As per the question, solving diagonally,

$
\begin{aligned}
R1C3 &= 30 - 13 - 10 \\\\
     &= 17 - 10 \\\\
     &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

Now lets find value of $A$, solving Row $1$

$
\begin{aligned}
9 + A + 7   &= 30 \\\\
9 + 7 + A   &= 30 \\\\
16 + A      &= 30 \\\\
A           &= 30 - 16 \\\\
A           &= 14
\end{aligned}
$

The number that replaces $A = 14$

</div>

<div class='question question'>

A number of shapes have been made with matches.

For this pattern:

![streatham-and-clapham-high-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-3-00.png](/assets/streatham-and-clapham-high-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-3-00.png "streatham-and-clapham-high-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-3-00.png")

How many matches will be in the $4^\text{th}$ shape?

- $26$
- $18$  
- $24$   
- $20$  
- $22$  

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 4 + (4-1) \times 6 \\\\
&= 4 + 3 \times 6 \\\\
&= 4 + 18 \\\\
&= 22
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>Q</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$22$

</div>
</div>

\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

The diagram shows Anthony the ant setting off for a walk in search of food. 
On this journey Anthony walked a certain distance, and turned right. 
Each time he turned right he walked $2 \ \text{metres}$ less than he did the time before.

![streatham-and-clapham-high-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-5-00.png](/assets/streatham-and-clapham-high-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-5-00.png "streatham-and-clapham-high-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-5-00.png")

On the last part of his journey before stopping he walked one metre. How far did he walk altogether?

- $38$
- $14$
- $51$
- $45$
- $49$  

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total distance covered}       &= 1 + 3 + 5 + 7 + 9 + 11 + 13 \\\\
                                    &= 49 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>E</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$49$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total distance covered}       &= 1 + 3 + 5 + 7 + 9 + 11 + 13 \\\\
                                    &= 49 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>E</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$49$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

When my age is divided by $2, 3, 4$ or $6$ there is always a remainder of $1$. 
But when divided by $7$ there is no remainder.
How old am I?

- $14$
- $49$ 
- $7$  
- $21$  
- $35$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Lcm of 2,3,4,6 = 12

Let the number $= 12n + 1$

As per the question,

$12n + 1$ should be divisible by $7$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Lets find out the value of n which satisfies the condition above.

| n |  12n + 1               |  Divisible by 7 |
|:-:|:----------------------:|:---------------:|
|$1$| $12 \times 1 + 1 = 13$ |        No       |
|$2$| $12 \times 2 + 1 = 10$ |        Yes      |
|$3$| $12 \times 3 + 1 = 37$ |        No       |
|$4$| $12 \times 4 + 1 = 49$ |        Yes      |

A quick check shows that $n = 4$ meets all the criteria above.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number} &= 12 \times n + 1 \\\\
              &= 12 \times 4 + 1 \\\\
              &= 48 + 1 \\\\
              &= 49
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$49$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Lcm of 2,3,4,6 = 12
<emptyline>
Let the number $= 12n + 1$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$12n + 1$ should be divisible by $7$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Lets find out the value of n which satisfies the condition above.
<emptyline>
| n |  12n + 1               |  Divisible by 7 |
|:-:|:----------------------:|:---------------:|
|$1$| $12 \times 1 + 1 = 13$ |        No       |
|$2$| $12 \times 2 + 1 = 10$ |        Yes      |
|$3$| $12 \times 3 + 1 = 37$ |        No       |
|$4$| $12 \times 4 + 1 = 49$ |        Yes      |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows that $n = 4$ meets all the criteria above.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number} &= 12 \times n + 1 \\\\
              &= 12 \times 4 + 1 \\\\
              &= 48 + 1 \\\\
              &= 49
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$49$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->

<div class='question question'>

Find two numbers which when multiplied together make a hundred. Neither of the two numbers can have the digit zero.

- $33, 67$
- $29, 17$
- $25, 4$
- $50, 2$  
- $11, 9$  

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

We can easily infer the value by using trial and error.

| Number 	| Product  	| Has $0?$ 	|
|:---:	|:---:	|:---:	|
| $33, 67$ 	| $33 \times 67 = \red{2211}$ 	| No 	|
| $29, 17$ 	| $29 \times 17 = \red{493}$ 	| No 	|
| $25, 4$ 	| $25 \times 4  = \green{100}$ 	    | No 	|
| $50, 2$ 	| $50 \times 2  = \red{100}$ 	| Yes   |
| $11, 9$ 	| $11 \times 9  = \red{99}$ 	| No 	|

Numbers $= 25, 4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$25, 4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>We can easily infer the value by using trial and error.
<emptyline>
| Number 	| Product  	| Has $0?$ 	|
|:---:	|:---:	|:---:	|
| $33, 67$ 	| $33 \times 67 = \rtq_katex_color_wrong_answer{2211}$ 	| No 	|
| $29, 17$ 	| $29 \times 17 = \rtq_katex_color_wrong_answer{493}$ 	| No 	|
| $25, 4$ 	| $25 \times 4  = \rtq_katex_color_answer{100}$ 	    | No 	|

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Find two numbers which when multiplied together make a thousand.
Once again, neither of the two numbers can have the digit zero in them.

- $100,10$
- $47,53$  
- $125, 8$   
- $99,111$  
- $25, 14$  

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

We can easily infer the value by using trial and error.

| Number 	| Product  	| Has $0?$ 	|
|:---:	|:---:	|:---:	|
| $100, 10$ 	| $100 \times 10 =  1000$ 	| Yes 	|
| $47, 53$ 	| $47 \times 53  =  2491$ 	| No 	|
| $125, 8$ 	| $125 \times 8  =  1000$ 	| No 	|
| $99, 111$ 	| $99 \times 111 = 10989$ 	| No 	|
| $25, 14$ 	| $25 \times 14  =  350$ 	| No 	|

Both options C and A multiply to $1000$. But Since, niether of the numbers should have digit $0$.

Numbers are $125, 8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$125, 8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>We can easily infer the value by using trial and error.
<emptyline>
| Number 	| Product  	| Has $0?$ 	|
|:---:	|:---:	|:---:	|
| $100, 10$ 	| $100 \times 10 =  1000$ 	| Yes 	|
| $47, 53$ 	| $47 \times 53  =  2491$ 	| No 	|
| $125, 8$ 	| $125 \times 8  =  1000$ 	| No 	|
| $99, 111$ 	| $99 \times 111 = 10989$ 	| No 	|
| $25, 14$ 	| $25 \times 14  =  350$ 	| No 	|
<emptyline>
Both options C and A multiply to $1000$. But Since, niether of the numbers should have digit $0$.
<emptyline>
Numbers are $125, 8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>C</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$125, 8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A cube with sides $3 \ \text{cm}$ is made from smaller cubes of side $1 \ \text{cm}$ as shown.

![streatham-and-clapham-high-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-9-00.png](/assets/streatham-and-clapham-high-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-9-00.png "streatham-and-clapham-high-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-9-00.png")

How many small cubes are used in making the bigger cube?

- $21$
- $27$
- $9$
- $18$
- $30$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of cubes} &= \dfrac {\text{Volume of bigger cube}} {\text{Volume of smaller cube}}  \\\\
                       &= \dfrac{3 \times 3 \times 3}{1 \times 1 \times 1} \\\\
                       &= 27
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$27$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of cubes} &= \dfrac {\text{Volume of bigger cube}} {\text{Volume of smaller cube}}  \\\\
                       &= \dfrac{3 \times 3 \times 3}{1 \times 1 \times 1} \\\\
                       &= 27
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$27$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

\text{Area of the shaded inner square} &= 8 \times 8 \\\\
                                       &= 64 \ \text{cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>G</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$64 \ \text{cm}^2$
</code></pre>
</div>
